# Upgrade external-agents E2E tests to match CLI repo

## Context

The external-agents e2e tests (`/Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/e2e/`) are protocol-level tests that exercise agent binary subcommands via stdin/stdout pipes. The CLI repo (`/Users/alisha/Projects/devenv/cli/e2e/`) has a much more mature e2e infrastructure with tmux-based interactive sessions, artifact capture, auth bootstrapping, and a `ForEachAgent` pattern. The user wants the external-agents e2e tests to match the CLI repo's approach.

**Key gaps to close:**
1. No tmux — lifecycle tests call `kiro-cli-chat` directly, can't test interactive flows
2. No artifacts — temp dirs are auto-cleaned, nothing survives for debugging
3. No auth bootstrap — lifecycle tests prompt for Kiro CLI login every time
4. No `ForEachAgent` pattern — tests hardcode the kiro agent
5. No `testify` — uses raw `t.Errorf` instead of assert/require
6. Flat package structure — everything in one `e2e` package vs sub-packages

## Plan

The external-agents e2e has its own `go.mod` at `e2e/go.mod`. New sub-packages are directories within it.

```
cd e2e && go get github.com/stretchr/testify@v1.11.1
```

**4.1 Rewrite `e2e/kiro_lifecycle_test.go`**
- Move to `e2e/tests/` package? **No** — the CLI repo uses a separate `tests` package because e2e tests are in the main module. Here, e2e has its own go.mod, so keep in root `e2e` package but import the new sub-packages.

- Replace all `NewLifecycleEnv` / `requireEntire` / `requireKiroCLI` calls with `testutil.ForEachAgent`
- Each test becomes:
```go
func TestLifecycle_SinglePromptManualCommit(t *testing.T) {
    testutil.ForEachAgent(t, 2*time.Minute, func(t *testing.T, s *testutil.RepoState, ctx context.Context) {
        _, err := s.RunPrompt(t, ctx, "Create a file called hello.txt with the content 'hello world'. Do not ask for confirmation.")
        if err != nil {
            t.Fatalf("prompt failed: %v", err)
        }
        testutil.AssertFileExists(t, s.Dir, "hello.txt")
        s.Git(t, "add", ".")
        s.Git(t, "commit", "-m", "add hello.txt")
        testutil.WaitForCheckpoint(t, s, 30*time.Second)
        cpID := testutil.AssertHasCheckpointTrailer(t, s.Dir, "HEAD")
        testutil.AssertCheckpointExists(t, s.Dir, cpID)
    })
}
```
- Add interactive session test using tmux:
```go
func TestLifecycle_InteractiveSession(t *testing.T) {
    testutil.ForEachAgent(t, 3*time.Minute, func(t *testing.T, s *testutil.RepoState, ctx context.Context) {
        session := s.StartSession(t, ctx)
        if session == nil {
            t.Skip("agent does not support interactive sessions")
        }
        s.Send(t, session, "Create a file called interactive.txt with 'hello'")
        s.WaitFor(t, session, s.Agent.PromptPattern(), 60*time.Second)
        testutil.WaitForFileExists(t, s.Dir, "interactive.txt", 10*time.Second)
        s.Git(t, "add", ".")
        s.Git(t, "commit", "-m", "interactive test")
        testutil.WaitForCheckpoint(t, s, 15*time.Second)
    })
}
```

## 2. /Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/e2e/setup_test.go

```go
//go:build e2e

package e2e

import (
	"fmt"
	"os"
	"os/exec"
	"path/filepath"
	"strings"
	"testing"
)

// agentBinaries maps agent names to their built binary paths.
var agentBinaries = map[string]string{}

func TestMain(m *testing.M) {
	tmpDir, err := os.MkdirTemp("", "e2e-agents-*")
	if err != nil {
		fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "failed to create temp dir: %v\n", err)
		os.Exit(1)
	}
	defer os.RemoveAll(tmpDir)

	agents, err := discoverAgents()
	if err != nil {
		fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "failed to discover agents: %v\n", err)
		os.Exit(1)
	}

	if len(agents) == 0 {
		fmt.Fprintln(os.Stderr, "no agents found in agents/ directory")
		os.Exit(1)
	}

	for _, agentDir := range agents {
		agentName := filepath.Base(agentDir)
		binPath := filepath.Join(tmpDir, agentName)
		// Each agent has its own go.mod, so build from the agent's directory
		agentAbsDir := filepath.Join(repoRoot(), agentDir)
		mainPkg := "./cmd/" + agentName

		fmt.Printf("Building %s...\n", agentName)
		cmd := exec.Command("go", "build", "-o", binPath, mainPkg)
		cmd.Dir = agentAbsDir
		cmd.Stdout = os.Stdout
		cmd.Stderr = os.Stderr
		if err := cmd.Run(); err != nil {
			fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "failed to build %s: %v\n", agentName, err)
			os.Exit(1)
		}

		agentBinaries[agentName] = binPath
		fmt.Printf("Built %s -> %s\n", agentName, binPath)
	}

	// Add the temp bin directory to PATH so that `entire enable` can discover
	// the agent binaries (e.g. entire-agent-kiro) during lifecycle tests.
	os.Setenv("PATH", tmpDir+string(os.PathListSeparator)+os.Getenv("PATH"))

	os.Exit(m.Run())
}

func discoverAgents() ([]string, error) {
	agentsDir := filepath.Join(repoRoot(), "agents")
	entries, err := os.ReadDir(agentsDir)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("read agents dir: %w", err)
	}

	var agents []string
	for _, entry := range entries {
		if !entry.IsDir() {
			continue
		}
		if !strings.HasPrefix(entry.Name(), "entire-agent-") {
			continue
		}
		// Verify it has a cmd/<name>/main.go
		mainFile := filepath.Join(agentsDir, entry.Name(), "cmd", entry.Name(), "main.go")
		if _, err := os.Stat(mainFile); err != nil {
			continue
		}
		agents = append(agents, filepath.Join("agents", entry.Name()))
	}
	return agents, nil
}

func repoRoot() string {
	// Walk up from e2e/ to find the repo root
	dir, err := os.Getwd()
	if err != nil {
		return ".."
	}
	// If we're in the e2e directory, go up one level
	if filepath.Base(dir) == "e2e" {
		return filepath.Dir(dir)
	}
	return dir
}
```

## 3. /Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/e2e/lifecycle.go

```go
//go:build e2e

package e2e

import (
	"fmt"
	"os"
	"os/exec"
	"path/filepath"
	"strings"
	"testing"
	"time"
)

// LifecycleEnv provides a full-lifecycle test environment: a git repo with
// `entire enable` already run, ready for agent prompts and checkpoint assertions.
type LifecycleEnv struct {
	Dir     string   // repo root (temp dir)
	HomeDir string   // isolated HOME
	Env     []string // environment variables for subprocesses
	Agent   string   // agent name (e.g. "kiro")
}

// NewLifecycleEnv creates a temp git repo, writes an initial commit, and runs
// `entire enable --agent <agentName>`. It also writes .entire/settings.json
// with external_agents enabled.
func NewLifecycleEnv(t *testing.T, agentName string) *LifecycleEnv {
	t.Helper()

	dir := t.TempDir()
	homeDir := t.TempDir()

	env := baseEnv(dir, homeDir)

	le := &LifecycleEnv{
		Dir:     dir,
		HomeDir: homeDir,
		Env:     env,
		Agent:   agentName,
	}

	// Initialize git repo with a seed commit.
	le.Git(t, "init")
	le.Git(t, "config", "user.email", "test@test.com")
	le.Git(t, "config", "user.name", "Test User")
	le.WriteFile(t, "README.md", "# test repo\n")
	le.Git(t, "add", ".")
	le.Git(t, "commit", "-m", "initial commit")

	// Write .entire/settings.json with external_agents enabled.
	le.MkdirAll(t, ".entire")
	le.WriteFile(t, ".entire/settings.json", `{"external_agents":true}`)

	// Run entire enable.
	EntireEnable(t, dir, agentName, env)

	return le
}

// Git runs a git command in the repo dir; fails the test on error.
func (le *LifecycleEnv) Git(t *testing.T, args ...string) {
	t.Helper()
	cmd := exec.Command("git", args...)
	cmd.Dir = le.Dir
	cmd.Env = le.Env
	if out, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("git %s failed: %v\n%s", strings.Join(args, " "), err, out)
	}
}

// GitOutput runs a git command and returns its combined output.
func (le *LifecycleEnv) GitOutput(t *testing.T, args ...string) string {
	t.Helper()
	cmd := exec.Command("git", args...)
	cmd.Dir = le.Dir
	cmd.Env = le.Env
	out, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("git %s failed: %v\n%s", strings.Join(args, " "), err, out)
	}
	return string(out)
}

// RunKiroPrompt runs `kiro-cli-chat chat --no-interactive --trust-all-tools --agent entire <prompt>`.
func (le *LifecycleEnv) RunKiroPrompt(t *testing.T, prompt string) error {
	t.Helper()
	bin, err := exec.LookPath("kiro-cli-chat")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("kiro-cli-chat not in PATH: %v", err)
	}
	cmd := exec.Command(bin, "chat", "--no-interactive", "--trust-all-tools", "--agent", "entire", prompt)
	cmd.Dir = le.Dir
	cmd.Env = le.Env
	out, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
	if err != nil {
		t.Logf("kiro-cli-chat output:\n%s", out)
		return fmt.Errorf("kiro-cli-chat: %w", err)
	}
	t.Logf("kiro-cli-chat output:\n%s", out)
	return nil
}

// FileExists checks whether relPath exists in the repo dir.
func (le *LifecycleEnv) FileExists(relPath string) bool {
	_, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(le.Dir, relPath))
	return err == nil
}

// WriteFile writes content to relPath inside the repo dir.
func (le *LifecycleEnv) WriteFile(t *testing.T, relPath, content string) {
	t.Helper()
	abs := filepath.Join(le.Dir, relPath)
	if err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Dir(abs), 0o750); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("mkdir for %s: %v", relPath, err)
	}
	if err := os.WriteFile(abs, []byte(content), 0o600); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("write %s: %v", relPath, err)
	}
}

// ReadFile reads a file from relPath inside the repo dir.
func (le *LifecycleEnv) ReadFile(t *testing.T, relPath string) string {
	t.Helper()
	data, err := os.ReadFile(filepath.Join(le.Dir, relPath))
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("read %s: %v", relPath, err)
	}
	return string(data)
}

// MkdirAll creates a directory (and parents) relative to the repo root.
func (le *LifecycleEnv) MkdirAll(t *testing.T, relPath string) {
	t.Helper()
	if err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Join(le.Dir, relPath), 0o750); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("mkdir %s: %v", relPath, err)
	}
}

// AbsPath returns the absolute path for a relative path in the repo.
func (le *LifecycleEnv) AbsPath(relPath string) string {
	return filepath.Join(le.Dir, relPath)
}

// WaitForCheckpoint polls for a checkpoint on the entire/checkpoints/v1 branch.
// It checks for new commits on that branch within the given timeout.
func WaitForCheckpoint(t *testing.T, le *LifecycleEnv, timeout time.Duration) {
	t.Helper()
	deadline := time.Now().Add(timeout)
	for time.Now().Before(deadline) {
		cmd := exec.Command("git", "rev-parse", "--verify", "entire/checkpoints/v1")
		cmd.Dir = le.Dir
		cmd.Env = le.Env
		if err := cmd.Run(); err == nil {
			t.Log("checkpoint branch found")
			return
		}
		time.Sleep(500 * time.Millisecond)
	}
	t.Fatal("timed out waiting for checkpoint branch entire/checkpoints/v1")
}

// GetCheckpointTrailer extracts the Entire-Checkpoint trailer from a commit ref.
func GetCheckpointTrailer(t *testing.T, le *LifecycleEnv, ref string) string {
	t.Helper()
	out := le.GitOutput(t, "log", "-1", "--format=%B", ref)
	for _, line := range strings.Split(out, "\n") {
		if strings.HasPrefix(line, "Entire-Checkpoint:") {
			return strings.TrimSpace(strings.TrimPrefix(line, "Entire-Checkpoint:"))
		}
	}
	return ""
}

// AssertFileExists fails the test if no file matches relPath in the repo.
func AssertFileExists(t *testing.T, le *LifecycleEnv, relPath string) {
	t.Helper()
	if !le.FileExists(relPath) {
		t.Errorf("expected file %s to exist", relPath)
	}
}

// requireEntire fails or skips the test if the entire binary is not available.
// When E2E_REQUIRE_LIFECYCLE=1 (set by `make test-e2e-lifecycle`), it fails
// instead of skipping so you know immediately that your environment is missing deps.
func requireEntire(t *testing.T) {
	t.Helper()
	if !EntireAvailable() {
		const msg = "entire binary not available (set E2E_ENTIRE_BIN or install entire)"
		if os.Getenv("E2E_REQUIRE_LIFECYCLE") == "1" {
			t.Fatal(msg)
		}
		t.Skip("skipping: " + msg)
	}
}

// requireKiroCLI fails or skips the test if kiro-cli-chat is not in PATH.
// When E2E_REQUIRE_LIFECYCLE=1, it fails instead of skipping.
func requireKiroCLI(t *testing.T) {
	t.Helper()
	if _, err := exec.LookPath("kiro-cli-chat"); err != nil {
		const msg = "kiro-cli-chat not in PATH"
		if os.Getenv("E2E_REQUIRE_LIFECYCLE") == "1" {
			t.Fatal(msg)
		}
		t.Skip("skipping: " + msg)
	}
}
```

## 4. /Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/e2e/entire.go

```go
//go:build e2e

package e2e

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"fmt"
	"os"
	"os/exec"
	"strings"
	"testing"
)

// RewindPoint represents a single entry from `entire rewind --list`.
type RewindPoint struct {
	ID              string `json:"id"`
	Message         string `json:"message"`
	IsLogsOnly      bool   `json:"is_logs_only"`
	CondensationID  string `json:"condensation_id"`
}

// EntireBinPath returns the path to the entire binary.
// It checks E2E_ENTIRE_BIN first, then falls back to looking in PATH.
func EntireBinPath() string {
	if p := os.Getenv("E2E_ENTIRE_BIN"); p != "" {
		return p
	}
	return "entire"
}

// EntireAvailable checks whether the entire binary can be found.
func EntireAvailable() bool {
	_, err := exec.LookPath(EntireBinPath())
	return err == nil
}

// EntireEnable runs `entire enable --agent <agent> --telemetry=false` in dir.
func EntireEnable(t *testing.T, dir string, agent string, env []string) {
	t.Helper()
	out := EntireRun(t, dir, env, "enable", "--agent", agent, "--telemetry=false")
	t.Logf("entire enable: %s", out)
}

// EntireDisable runs `entire disable` in dir.
func EntireDisable(t *testing.T, dir string, env []string) {
	t.Helper()
	out := EntireRun(t, dir, env, "disable")
	t.Logf("entire disable: %s", out)
}

// EntireRewindList parses the JSON output of `entire rewind --list`.
func EntireRewindList(t *testing.T, dir string, env []string) []RewindPoint {
	t.Helper()
	out := EntireRun(t, dir, env, "rewind", "--list")

	var points []RewindPoint
	if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(out), &points); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("parse rewind --list JSON: %v\noutput: %s", err, out)
	}
	return points
}

// EntireRewind runs `entire rewind --to <id>`.
func EntireRewind(t *testing.T, dir string, env []string, id string) {
	t.Helper()
	out := EntireRun(t, dir, env, "rewind", "--to", id)
	t.Logf("entire rewind --to %s: %s", id, out)
}

// EntireRun executes the entire binary with the given args in dir. It fails
// the test on non-zero exit.
func EntireRun(t *testing.T, dir string, env []string, args ...string) string {
	t.Helper()
	out, err := EntireRunErr(dir, env, args...)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("entire %s failed: %v\noutput: %s", strings.Join(args, " "), err, out)
	}
	return out
}

// EntireRunErr executes the entire binary and returns stdout+stderr combined.
// It does not fail the test on error — useful for expected-failure cases.
func EntireRunErr(dir string, env []string, args ...string) (string, error) {
	bin := EntireBinPath()
	cmd := exec.Command(bin, args...)
	cmd.Dir = dir
	if env != nil {
		cmd.Env = env
	}
	out, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
	if err != nil {
		return string(out), fmt.Errorf("%s %s: %w", bin, strings.Join(args, " "), err)
	}
	return string(out), nil
}
```

```go
//go:build e2e

package e2e

import (
	"os"
	"os/exec"
	"path/filepath"
	"testing"
	"time"
)

// TestLifecycle_SinglePromptManualCommit verifies the basic flow:
// agent creates a file → git add + commit → checkpoint exists with trailer.
func TestLifecycle_SinglePromptManualCommit(t *testing.T) {
	requireEntire(t)
	requireKiroCLI(t)
	t.Parallel()

	env := NewLifecycleEnv(t, "kiro")

	// Ask the agent to create a file.
	if err := env.RunKiroPrompt(t, "Create a file called hello.txt with the content 'hello world'"); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("prompt failed: %v", err)
	}

	AssertFileExists(t, env, "hello.txt")

	// Manually commit so the checkpoint hook fires.
	env.Git(t, "add", ".")
	env.Git(t, "commit", "-m", "add hello.txt")

	// Wait for the checkpoint branch to appear.
	WaitForCheckpoint(t, env, 30*time.Second)

	// Verify the checkpoint has the Entire-Checkpoint trailer.
	trailer := GetCheckpointTrailer(t, env, "entire/checkpoints/v1")
	if trailer == "" {
		t.Error("expected Entire-Checkpoint trailer on checkpoint commit")
	}
}

// TestLifecycle_MultiplePromptsManualCommit sends two prompts, commits once,
// and verifies the checkpoint covers both files.
func TestLifecycle_MultiplePromptsManualCommit(t *testing.T) {
	requireEntire(t)
	requireKiroCLI(t)
	t.Parallel()

	env := NewLifecycleEnv(t, "kiro")

	if err := env.RunKiroPrompt(t, "Create a file called foo.txt containing 'foo'"); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("first prompt failed: %v", err)
	}
	if err := env.RunKiroPrompt(t, "Create a file called bar.txt containing 'bar'"); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("second prompt failed: %v", err)
	}

	AssertFileExists(t, env, "foo.txt")
	AssertFileExists(t, env, "bar.txt")

	env.Git(t, "add", ".")
	env.Git(t, "commit", "-m", "add foo and bar")

	WaitForCheckpoint(t, env, 30*time.Second)
}

// TestLifecycle_DetectAndEnable verifies that `entire enable --agent kiro`
// works when .kiro/ already exists in the repo.
func TestLifecycle_DetectAndEnable(t *testing.T) {
	requireEntire(t)
	t.Parallel()

	dir := t.TempDir()
	homeDir := t.TempDir()
	env := baseEnv(dir, homeDir)

	// Init a git repo with a .kiro/ directory.
	runGit(t, dir, env, "init")
	runGit(t, dir, env, "config", "user.email", "test@test.com")
	runGit(t, dir, env, "config", "user.name", "Test User")
	if err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Join(dir, ".kiro"), 0o750); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("mkdir .kiro: %v", err)
	}
	if err := os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(dir, "README.md"), []byte("# test\n"), 0o600); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("write readme: %v", err)
	}
	runGit(t, dir, env, "add", ".")
	runGit(t, dir, env, "commit", "-m", "initial")

	// Enable should succeed because .kiro/ is present.
	EntireEnable(t, dir, "kiro", env)

	// Verify .entire/ directory was created.
	if _, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(dir, ".entire")); err != nil {
		t.Errorf("expected .entire/ directory after enable: %v", err)
	}
}

// TestLifecycle_HooksInstalledAfterEnable verifies that hooks are installed
// after running `entire enable`.
func TestLifecycle_HooksInstalledAfterEnable(t *testing.T) {
	requireEntire(t)
	t.Parallel()

	env := NewLifecycleEnv(t, "kiro")

	// The agent binary's are-hooks-installed subcommand should confirm hooks are present.
	binPath, ok := agentBinaries["entire-agent-kiro"]
	if !ok {
		t.Skip("entire-agent-kiro binary not built")
	}

	runner := &AgentRunner{
		BinaryPath: binPath,
		Env:        env.Env,
	}

	var resp struct {
		Installed bool `json:"installed"`
	}
	runner.RunJSON(t, &resp, "", "are-hooks-installed")

	if !resp.Installed {
		t.Error("hooks should be installed after entire enable")
	}
}

// TestLifecycle_RewindPreCommit verifies the rewind flow before a commit:
// create file A → snapshot rewind points → create file B → rewind → file A exists, file B gone.
func TestLifecycle_RewindPreCommit(t *testing.T) {
	requireEntire(t)
	requireKiroCLI(t)
	t.Parallel()

	env := NewLifecycleEnv(t, "kiro")

	// Create first file via agent.
	if err := env.RunKiroPrompt(t, "Create a file called alpha.txt with content 'alpha'"); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("first prompt failed: %v", err)
	}
	AssertFileExists(t, env, "alpha.txt")

	// Commit so we have a checkpoint to rewind to.
	env.Git(t, "add", ".")
	env.Git(t, "commit", "-m", "add alpha")
	WaitForCheckpoint(t, env, 30*time.Second)

	// Snapshot rewind points.
	points := EntireRewindList(t, env.Dir, env.Env)
	if len(points) == 0 {
		t.Fatal("expected at least one rewind point after first commit")
	}
	rewindTarget := points[0].ID

	// Create a second file.
	if err := env.RunKiroPrompt(t, "Create a file called beta.txt with content 'beta'"); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("second prompt failed: %v", err)
	}
	AssertFileExists(t, env, "beta.txt")

	// Rewind to the point after alpha but before beta.
	EntireRewind(t, env.Dir, env.Env, rewindTarget)

	// alpha.txt should still exist, beta.txt should be gone.
	if !env.FileExists("alpha.txt") {
		t.Error("alpha.txt should exist after rewind")
	}
	if env.FileExists("beta.txt") {
		t.Error("beta.txt should not exist after rewind")
	}
}

// TestLifecycle_RewindAfterCommit verifies that rewind points are available
// after a git commit and that rewinding restores the correct state.
func TestLifecycle_RewindAfterCommit(t *testing.T) {
	requireEntire(t)
	requireKiroCLI(t)
	t.Parallel()

	env := NewLifecycleEnv(t, "kiro")

	// First prompt + commit.
	if err := env.RunKiroPrompt(t, "Create a file called first.txt with content 'first'"); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("first prompt failed: %v", err)
	}
	env.Git(t, "add", ".")
	env.Git(t, "commit", "-m", "add first.txt")
	WaitForCheckpoint(t, env, 30*time.Second)

	pointsAfterFirst := EntireRewindList(t, env.Dir, env.Env)
	if len(pointsAfterFirst) == 0 {
		t.Fatal("expected rewind points after first commit")
	}

	// Second prompt + commit.
	if err := env.RunKiroPrompt(t, "Create a file called second.txt with content 'second'"); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("second prompt failed: %v", err)
	}
	env.Git(t, "add", ".")
	env.Git(t, "commit", "-m", "add second.txt")
	WaitForCheckpoint(t, env, 30*time.Second)

	pointsAfterSecond := EntireRewindList(t, env.Dir, env.Env)
	if len(pointsAfterSecond) <= len(pointsAfterFirst) {
		t.Error("expected more rewind points after second commit")
	}

	// Rewind to the state after the first commit.
	EntireRewind(t, env.Dir, env.Env, pointsAfterFirst[0].ID)

	if !env.FileExists("first.txt") {
		t.Error("first.txt should exist after rewind")
	}
	if env.FileExists("second.txt") {
		t.Error("second.txt should not exist after rewind")
	}
}

// TestLifecycle_SessionPersistence verifies that a session file is created in
// .entire/tmp/ after running a prompt.
func TestLifecycle_SessionPersistence(t *testing.T) {
	requireEntire(t)
	requireKiroCLI(t)
	t.Parallel()

	env := NewLifecycleEnv(t, "kiro")

	if err := env.RunKiroPrompt(t, "Create a file called session-test.txt with content 'test'"); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("prompt failed: %v", err)
	}

	// Check that at least one session file exists in .entire/tmp/.
	tmpDir := filepath.Join(env.Dir, ".entire", "tmp")
	entries, err := os.ReadDir(tmpDir)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("read .entire/tmp/: %v", err)
	}

	hasSession := false
	for _, entry := range entries {
		if filepath.Ext(entry.Name()) == ".json" {
			hasSession = true
			break
		}
	}
	if !hasSession {
		t.Error("expected at least one .json session file in .entire/tmp/")
	}
}

// runGit is a standalone helper for running git outside of a LifecycleEnv.
func runGit(t *testing.T, dir string, env []string, args ...string) {
	t.Helper()
	cmd := exec.Command("git", args...)
	cmd.Dir = dir
	cmd.Env = env
	if out, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("git %s: %v\n%s", args[0], err, out)
	}
}
```

## 6. /Users/alisha/Projects/external-agents/e2e/harness.go

```go
//go:build e2e

package e2e

import (
	"bytes"
	"encoding/json"
	"errors"
	"os/exec"
	"testing"
)

// CommandResult holds the output of a binary invocation.
type CommandResult struct {
	Stdout   []byte
	Stderr   []byte
	ExitCode int
	Err      error
}

// AgentRunner invokes an agent binary with subcommands.
type AgentRunner struct {
	BinaryPath string
	Env        []string
}

// Run executes the agent binary with the given subcommand, args, and optional stdin.
func (r *AgentRunner) Run(stdin string, subcommand string, args ...string) CommandResult {
	cmdArgs := append([]string{subcommand}, args...)
	cmd := exec.Command(r.BinaryPath, cmdArgs...)
	cmd.Stdin = bytes.NewBufferString(stdin)
	cmd.Env = r.Env

	var stdout, stderr bytes.Buffer
	cmd.Stdout = &stdout
	cmd.Stderr = &stderr

	err := cmd.Run()
	exitCode := 0
	if err != nil {
		var exitErr *exec.ExitError
		if errors.As(err, &exitErr) {
			exitCode = exitErr.ExitCode()
		} else {
			exitCode = -1
		}
	}

	return CommandResult{
		Stdout:   stdout.Bytes(),
		Stderr:   stderr.Bytes(),
		ExitCode: exitCode,
		Err:      err,
	}
}

// RunJSON executes the subcommand and JSON-decodes stdout into dest.
func (r *AgentRunner) RunJSON(t *testing.T, dest any, stdin string, subcommand string, args ...string) CommandResult {
	t.Helper()
	result := r.Run(stdin, subcommand, args...)
	if result.ExitCode != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("%s %s failed (exit %d): %s", r.BinaryPath, subcommand, result.ExitCode, result.Stderr)
	}
	if err := json.Unmarshal(result.Stdout, dest); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("failed to decode JSON from %s %s: %v\nstdout: %s", r.BinaryPath, subcommand, err, result.Stdout)
	}
	return result
}

// MustSucceed asserts the subcommand exits with code 0.
func (r *AgentRunner) MustSucceed(t *testing.T, stdin string, subcommand string, args ...string) CommandResult {
	t.Helper()
	result := r.Run(stdin, subcommand, args...)
	if result.ExitCode != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("%s %s: expected exit 0, got %d\nstderr: %s", r.BinaryPath, subcommand, result.ExitCode, result.Stderr)
	}
	return result
}

// MustFail asserts the subcommand exits with a non-zero code.
func (r *AgentRunner) MustFail(t *testing.T, stdin string, subcommand string, args ...string) CommandResult {
	t.Helper()
	result := r.Run(stdin, subcommand, args...)
	if result.ExitCode == 0 {
		t.Fatalf("%s %s: expected non-zero exit, got 0\nstdout: %s", r.BinaryPath, subcommand, result.Stdout)
	}
	return result
}
```

```go
//go:build e2e

package e2e

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"fmt"
	"os"
	"os/exec"
	"path/filepath"
	"testing"
)

// TestEnv provides an isolated filesystem environment for E2E tests.
type TestEnv struct {
	t       *testing.T
	Dir     string
	HomeDir string
	Runner  *AgentRunner
}

// NewTestEnv creates a bare test environment with ENTIRE_REPO_ROOT and isolated HOME.
func NewTestEnv(t *testing.T, agentName string) *TestEnv {
	t.Helper()

	binPath, ok := agentBinaries[agentName]
	if !ok {
		t.Fatalf("agent binary not found: %s (available: %v)", agentName, agentBinaryNames())
	}

	dir := t.TempDir()
	homeDir := t.TempDir()

	env := baseEnv(dir, homeDir)

	return &TestEnv{
		t:       t,
		Dir:     dir,
		HomeDir: homeDir,
		Runner: &AgentRunner{
			BinaryPath: binPath,
			Env:        env,
		},
	}
}

// NewKiroTestEnv creates a test environment with .kiro/ and .entire/tmp/ directories.
func NewKiroTestEnv(t *testing.T) *TestEnv {
	t.Helper()
	te := NewTestEnv(t, "entire-agent-kiro")
	te.MkdirAll(".kiro")
	te.MkdirAll(".entire/tmp")
	return te
}

// NewKiroGitEnv creates a Kiro test environment with git init.
func NewKiroGitEnv(t *testing.T) *TestEnv {
	t.Helper()
	te := NewKiroTestEnv(t)
	te.GitInit()
	return te
}

// WriteFile writes content to a path relative to the test environment root.
func (e *TestEnv) WriteFile(relPath, content string) {
	e.t.Helper()
	abs := filepath.Join(e.Dir, relPath)
	if err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Dir(abs), 0o750); err != nil {
		e.t.Fatalf("mkdir for %s: %v", relPath, err)
	}
	if err := os.WriteFile(abs, []byte(content), 0o600); err != nil {
		e.t.Fatalf("write %s: %v", relPath, err)
	}
}

// WriteJSON writes a JSON-encoded value to a path relative to the test root.
func (e *TestEnv) WriteJSON(relPath string, v any) {
	e.t.Helper()
	data, err := json.Marshal(v)
	if err != nil {
		e.t.Fatalf("marshal JSON for %s: %v", relPath, err)
	}
	e.WriteFile(relPath, string(data))
}

// ReadFile reads a file relative to the test environment root.
func (e *TestEnv) ReadFile(relPath string) string {
	e.t.Helper()
	data, err := os.ReadFile(filepath.Join(e.Dir, relPath))
	if err != nil {
		e.t.Fatalf("read %s: %v", relPath, err)
	}
	return string(data)
}

// FileExists checks whether a relative path exists in the test environment.
func (e *TestEnv) FileExists(relPath string) bool {
	_, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(e.Dir, relPath))
	return err == nil
}

// MkdirAll creates a directory (and parents) relative to the test root.
func (e *TestEnv) MkdirAll(relPath string) {
	e.t.Helper()
	if err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Join(e.Dir, relPath), 0o750); err != nil {
		e.t.Fatalf("mkdir %s: %v", relPath, err)
	}
}

// GitInit initializes a git repo in the test environment root.
func (e *TestEnv) GitInit() {
	e.t.Helper()
	cmd := exec.Command("git", "init")
	cmd.Dir = e.Dir
	cmd.Env = e.Runner.Env
	if out, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
		e.t.Fatalf("git init failed: %v\n%s", err, out)
	}
}

// AbsPath returns the absolute path for a relative path in the test environment.
func (e *TestEnv) AbsPath(relPath string) string {
	return filepath.Join(e.Dir, relPath)
}

func baseEnv(repoRoot, homeDir string) []string {
	return []string{
		fmt.Sprintf("ENTIRE_REPO_ROOT=%s", repoRoot),
		fmt.Sprintf("HOME=%s", homeDir),
		fmt.Sprintf("PATH=%s", os.Getenv("PATH")),
		"LANG=en_US.UTF-8",
	}
}

func agentBinaryNames() []string {
	names := make([]string, 0, len(agentBinaries))
	for name := range agentBinaries {
		names = append(names, name)
	}
	return names
}
```

```go
//go:build e2e

package e2e

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"testing"
)

// HookInput builds stdin payloads for hook-related subcommands.
type HookInput struct {
	HookType   string                 `json:"hook_type,omitempty"`
	SessionID  string                 `json:"session_id,omitempty"`
	SessionRef string                 `json:"session_ref,omitempty"`
	Timestamp  string                 `json:"timestamp,omitempty"`
	UserPrompt string                 `json:"user_prompt,omitempty"`
	ToolName   string                 `json:"tool_name,omitempty"`
	ToolUseID  string                 `json:"tool_use_id,omitempty"`
	ToolInput  json.RawMessage        `json:"tool_input,omitempty"`
	RawData    map[string]interface{} `json:"raw_data,omitempty"`
}

// JSON returns the JSON-encoded string for use as stdin.
func (h HookInput) JSON(t *testing.T) string {
	t.Helper()
	data, err := json.Marshal(h)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("marshal HookInput: %v", err)
	}
	return string(data)
}

// ParseHookInput builds stdin payloads for the parse-hook subcommand.
type ParseHookInput struct {
	HookEventName string          `json:"hook_event_name,omitempty"`
	CWD           string          `json:"cwd,omitempty"`
	Prompt        string          `json:"prompt,omitempty"`
	ToolName      string          `json:"tool_name,omitempty"`
	ToolInput     json.RawMessage `json:"tool_input,omitempty"`
}

// JSON returns the JSON-encoded string.
func (p ParseHookInput) JSON(t *testing.T) string {
	t.Helper()
	data, err := json.Marshal(p)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("marshal ParseHookInput: %v", err)
	}
	return string(data)
}

// KiroTranscript builds Kiro-format transcript files for testing.
type KiroTranscript struct {
	ConversationID string              `json:"conversation_id"`
	History        []kiroHistoryEntry  `json:"history"`
}

type kiroHistoryEntry struct {
	User      kiroUserMessage `json:"user"`
	Assistant json.RawMessage `json:"assistant"`
}

type kiroUserMessage struct {
	Content   json.RawMessage `json:"content"`
	Timestamp string          `json:"timestamp,omitempty"`
}

// NewKiroTranscript creates a new transcript builder.
func NewKiroTranscript(id string) *KiroTranscript {
	return &KiroTranscript{ConversationID: id}
}

func marshalPromptContent(prompt string) json.RawMessage {
	content, _ := json.Marshal(map[string]interface{}{
		"Prompt": map[string]string{"prompt": prompt},
	})
	return content
}

// AddPrompt adds a user prompt entry with no assistant response.
func (kt *KiroTranscript) AddPrompt(prompt string) *KiroTranscript {
	kt.History = append(kt.History, kiroHistoryEntry{
		User: kiroUserMessage{Content: marshalPromptContent(prompt)},
	})
	return kt
}

// AddPromptWithFileEdit adds a user prompt paired with an assistant response that contains a file edit tool use.
func (kt *KiroTranscript) AddPromptWithFileEdit(prompt, filePath string) *KiroTranscript {
	toolUse := map[string]interface{}{
		"ToolUse": map[string]interface{}{
			"message_id": "msg-001",
			"tool_uses": []map[string]interface{}{
				{
					"id":   "tool-001",
					"name": "fs_write",
					"args": map[string]string{"path": filePath},
				},
			},
		},
	}
	assistantContent, _ := json.Marshal(toolUse)

	kt.History = append(kt.History, kiroHistoryEntry{
		User:      kiroUserMessage{Content: marshalPromptContent(prompt)},
		Assistant: assistantContent,
	})
	return kt
}

// AddResponse adds a user prompt paired with an assistant text response.
func (kt *KiroTranscript) AddResponse(prompt, response string) *KiroTranscript {
	userContent := marshalPromptContent(prompt)

	responseContent := map[string]interface{}{
		"Response": map[string]interface{}{
			"message_id": "msg-resp",
			"content":    response,
		},
	}
	assistantContent, _ := json.Marshal(responseContent)

	kt.History = append(kt.History, kiroHistoryEntry{
		User:      kiroUserMessage{Content: userContent},
		Assistant: assistantContent,
	})
	return kt
}

// JSON returns the JSON-encoded transcript string.
func (kt *KiroTranscript) JSON(t *testing.T) string {
	t.Helper()
	data, err := json.Marshal(kt)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("marshal KiroTranscript: %v", err)
	}
	return string(data)
}

// WriteToFile writes the transcript to a file and returns the absolute path.
func (kt *KiroTranscript) WriteToFile(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, relPath string) string {
	t.Helper()
	env.WriteFile(relPath, kt.JSON(t))
	return env.AbsPath(relPath)
}
```

```go
		} `json:"capabilities"`
	}

func TestKiro_FormatResumeCommand(t *testing.T) {
	t.Parallel()
	env := NewKiroTestEnv(t)

	var resp struct {
		Command string `json:"command"`
	}
	env.Runner.RunJSON(t, &resp, "", "format-resume-command", "-session-id", "session-xyz")

	if resp.Command == "" {
		t.Error("command should not be empty")
	}
	if !strings.Contains(resp.Command, "resume") {
		t.Errorf("command %q should contain 'resume'", resp.Command)
	}
}

func TestKiro_UnknownSubcommand(t *testing.T) {
	t.Parallel()
	env := NewKiroTestEnv(t)

	result := env.Runner.MustFail(t, "", "nonexistent-command")
	if !strings.Contains(string(result.Stderr), "unknown subcommand") {
		t.Errorf("stderr should mention 'unknown subcommand', got: %s", result.Stderr)
	}
}

func TestKiro_NoSubcommand(t *testing.T) {
	t.Parallel()
	env := NewKiroTestEnv(t)

	// Run with no args — the binary expects at least a subcommand
	result := env.Runner.Run("", "")
	// The binary wraps os.Args[1] so passing empty string gives "unknown subcommand: "
	// which exits non-zero. Either way it should not succeed cleanly.
	// Actually passing "" as subcommand will invoke the binary with "" as the arg.
	if result.ExitCode == 0 {
		t.Error("expected non-zero exit for empty subcommand")
	}
}

// --- Install + Idempotency ---

func TestKiro_InstallHooks_Idempotent(t *testing.T) {
	t.Parallel()
	env := NewKiroTestEnv(t)

	// First install
	var resp1 struct {
		HooksInstalled int `json:"hooks_installed"`
	}
	env.Runner.RunJSON(t, &resp1, "", "install-hooks")
	if resp1.HooksInstalled == 0 {
		t.Fatal("first install should install hooks")
	}

	// Second install should be a no-op (returns 0 installed)
	var resp2 struct {
		HooksInstalled int `json:"hooks_installed"`
	}
	env.Runner.RunJSON(t, &resp2, "", "install-hooks")
	if resp2.HooksInstalled != 0 {
		t.Errorf("second install should be idempotent (0 hooks), got %d", resp2.HooksInstalled)
	}
}
```

```
     1→package agents
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"os"
     6→	"strconv"
     7→	"strings"
     8→	"time"
     9→)
    10→
    11→type Output struct {
    12→	Command  string
    13→	Stdout   string
    14→	Stderr   string
    15→	ExitCode int
    16→}
    17→
    18→type Option func(*runConfig)
    19→type runConfig struct {
    20→	Model          string
    21→	PermissionMode string
    22→	PromptTimeout  time.Duration
    23→}
    24→
    25→func WithModel(model string) Option {
    26→	return func(c *runConfig) { c.Model = model }
    27→}
    28→
    29→func WithPermissionMode(mode string) Option {
    30→	return func(c *runConfig) { c.PermissionMode = mode }
    31→}
    32→
    33→func WithPromptTimeout(d time.Duration) Option {
    34→	return func(c *runConfig) { c.PromptTimeout = d }
    35→}
    36→
    37→type Agent interface {
    38→	Name() string
    39→	// Binary returns the CLI binary name (e.g. "claude", "gemini").
    40→	Binary() string
    41→	EntireAgent() string
    42→	PromptPattern() string
    43→	// TimeoutMultiplier returns a factor applied to per-test timeouts.
    44→	// Slower agents (e.g. Gemini) return values > 1.
    45→	TimeoutMultiplier() float64
    46→	RunPrompt(ctx context.Context, dir string, prompt string, opts ...Option) (Output, error)
    47→	StartSession(ctx context.Context, dir string) (Session, error)
    48→	// Bootstrap performs one-time CI setup (auth config, warmup, etc.).
    49→	// Called before any tests run. Implementations should be idempotent.
    50→	Bootstrap() error
    51→	// IsTransientError returns true if the error from RunPrompt looks like
    52→	// a transient API failure (e.g. 500, rate limit, network error) that
    53→	// is worth retrying.
    54→	IsTransientError(out Output, err error) bool
    55→}
    56→
    57→type Session interface {
    58→	Send(input string) error
    59→	WaitFor(pattern string, timeout time.Duration) (string, error)
    60→	Capture() string
    61→	Close() error
    62→}
    63→
    64→// ExternalAgent is an optional interface for agents discovered via the
    65→// external agent protocol (entire-agent-* binaries). SetupRepo uses this
    66→// to pre-configure external_agents in settings before running `entire enable`.
    67→type ExternalAgent interface {
    68→	IsExternalAgent() bool
    69→}
    70→
    71→var registry []Agent
    72→var gates = map[string]chan struct{}{}
    73→
    74→func Register(a Agent) {
    75→	registry = append(registry, a)
    76→}
    77→
    78→// RegisterGate sets a concurrency limit for an agent's tests.
    79→// Tests call AcquireSlot/ReleaseSlot to respect this limit.
    80→// The limit can be overridden via E2E_CONCURRENT_TEST_LIMIT.
    81→func RegisterGate(name string, defaultMax int) {
    82→	max := defaultMax
    83→	if v, err := strconv.Atoi(os.Getenv("E2E_CONCURRENT_TEST_LIMIT")); err == nil && v > 0 {
    84→		max = v
    85→	}
    86→	gates[name] = make(chan struct{}, max)
    87→}
    88→
    89→// AcquireSlot blocks until a test slot is available for the agent or the
    90→// context is cancelled. Returns a non-nil error if the context expires
    91→// before a slot opens.
    92→func AcquireSlot(ctx context.Context, a Agent) error {
    93→	g, ok := gates[a.Name()]
    94→	if !ok {
    95→		return nil
    96→	}
    97→	select {
    98→	case g <- struct{}{}:
    99→		return nil
   100→	case <-ctx.Done():
   101→		return ctx.Err()
   102→	}
   103→}
   104→
   105→// ReleaseSlot frees a test slot for the agent.
   106→func ReleaseSlot(a Agent) {
   107→	if g, ok := gates[a.Name()]; ok {
   108→		<-g
   109→	}
   110→}
   111→
   112→func All() []Agent {
   113→	return registry
   114→}
   115→
   116→// filterEnv returns env with entries matching any of the given variable names
   117→// removed. Used to strip test-only overrides (e.g. ENTIRE_TEST_TTY) from agent
   118→// processes so they exercise real detection paths.
   119→func filterEnv(env []string, names ...string) []string {
   120→	out := make([]string, 0, len(env))
   121→	for _, e := range env {
   122→		skip := false
   123→		for _, name := range names {
   124→			if strings.HasPrefix(e, name+"=") {
   125→				skip = true
   126→				break
   127→			}
   128→		}
   129→		if !skip {
   130→			out = append(out, e)
   131→		}
   132→	}
   133→	return out
   134→}
   135→
```

```
     1→package agents
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"fmt"
     5→	"os"
     6→	"os/exec"
     7→	"regexp"
     8→	"strings"
     9→	"time"
    10→)
    11→
    12→// TmuxSession implements Session using tmux for PTY-based interactive agents.
    13→type TmuxSession struct {
    14→	name         string
    15→	stableAtSend string   // stable content snapshot when Send was last called
    16→	cleanups     []func() // run on Close
    17→}
    18→
    19→// OnClose registers a function to run when the session is closed.
    20→func (s *TmuxSession) OnClose(fn func()) {
    21→	s.cleanups = append(s.cleanups, fn)
    22→}
    23→
    24→// NewTmuxSession creates a new tmux session running the given command in dir.
    25→// unsetEnv lists environment variable names to strip from the session.
    26→//
    27→// The command is wrapped with `env` to propagate PATH from the current process.
    28→// tmux sessions inherit the tmux server's environment (not the client's), so
    29→// without this, binaries added to PATH by the test runner (e.g., freshly built
    30→// `entire` and `vogon`) would not be found inside the session.
    31→func NewTmuxSession(name string, dir string, unsetEnv []string, command string, args ...string) (*TmuxSession, error) {
    32→	s := &TmuxSession{name: name}
    33→
    34→	tmuxArgs := []string{"new-session", "-d", "-s", name, "-c", dir}
    35→	// Build a shell command string using `env` to:
    36→	//   1. Propagate PATH from the current process (tmux server may have stale PATH)
    37→	//   2. Unset variables listed in unsetEnv
    38→	// All arguments are shell-quoted to prevent injection or splitting.
    39→	// PATH is always forwarded from the current process so that the freshly-built
    40→	// "entire" binary (prepended to PATH by main_test.go) is available inside the
    41→	// tmux session. Without this, tmux inherits the server's environment which may
    42→	// have an older binary (or none at all).
    43→	var parts []string
    44→	parts = append(parts, "env")
    45→	// Options (-u) must precede variable assignments for BSD env on macOS.
    46→	for _, v := range unsetEnv {
    47→		parts = append(parts, "-u", shellQuote(v))
    48→	}
    49→	parts = append(parts, "PATH="+shellQuote(os.Getenv("PATH")))
    50→	parts = append(parts, shellQuote(command))
    51→	for _, a := range args {
    52→		parts = append(parts, shellQuote(a))
    53→	}
    54→	tmuxArgs = append(tmuxArgs, strings.Join(parts, " "))
    55→
    56→	cmd := exec.Command("tmux", tmuxArgs...)
    57→	if out, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
    58→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("tmux new-session: %w\n%s", err, out)
    59→	}
    60→	// Keep the pane around after the command exits so we can capture error output.
    61→	setCmd := exec.Command("tmux", "set-option", "-t", name, "remain-on-exit", "on")
    62→	_ = setCmd.Run()
    63→	return s, nil
    64→}
    65→
    66→func (s *TmuxSession) Send(input string) error {
    67→	preSend := stableContent(s.Capture())
    68→	// Send text and Enter separately — Claude's TUI can swallow Enter
    69→	// if it arrives before the input handler finishes processing the text.
    70→	if err := s.SendKeys(input); err != nil {
    71→		return err
    72→	}
    73→	time.Sleep(200 * time.Millisecond)
    74→	if err := s.SendKeys("Enter"); err != nil {
    75→		return err
    76→	}
    77→
    78→	// Wait for the terminal to reflect the echoed input, then snapshot.
    79→	// This ensures WaitFor compares against post-echo content, preventing
    80→	// false matches on prompt characters (e.g. ❯) in the echoed input.
    81→	deadline := time.Now().Add(5 * time.Second)
    82→	for time.Now().Before(deadline) {
    83→		time.Sleep(200 * time.Millisecond)
    84→		current := stableContent(s.Capture())
    85→		if current != preSend {
    86→			s.stableAtSend = current
    87→			return nil
    88→		}
    89→	}
    90→	s.stableAtSend = stableContent(s.Capture())
    91→	return nil
    92→}
    93→
    94→// SendKeys sends raw tmux key names without appending Enter.
    95→func (s *TmuxSession) SendKeys(keys ...string) error {
    96→	args := append([]string{"send-keys", "-t", s.name}, keys...)
    97→	cmd := exec.Command("tmux", args...)
    98→	out, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
    99→	if err != nil {
   100→		return fmt.Errorf("tmux send-keys: %w\n%s", err, out)
   101→	}
   102→	return nil
   103→}
   104→
   105→const (
   106→	settleTime   = 2 * time.Second
   107→	pollInterval = 500 * time.Millisecond
   108→)
   109→
   110→// stableContent returns the content with the last few lines stripped,
   111→// so that TUI status bar updates don't prevent the settle timer.
   112→func stableContent(content string) string {
   113→	lines := strings.Split(content, "\n")
   114→	if len(lines) > 3 {
   115→		lines = lines[:len(lines)-3]
   116→	}
   117→	return strings.Join(lines, "\n")
   118→}
   119→
   120→func (s *TmuxSession) WaitFor(pattern string, timeout time.Duration) (string, error) {
   121→	re, err := regexp.Compile(pattern)
   122→	if err != nil {
   123→		return "", fmt.Errorf("invalid pattern: %w", err)
   124→	}
   125→
   126→	deadline := time.Now().Add(timeout)
   127→	var matchedAt time.Time
   128→	var lastStable string
   129→	contentChanged := s.stableAtSend == "" // skip change requirement for initial waits
   130→
   131→	for time.Now().Before(deadline) {
   132→		content := s.Capture()
   133→		stable := stableContent(content)
   134→
   135→		// Bail early if the process has exited and the pattern doesn't match.
   136→		// remain-on-exit keeps the pane alive, so without this check we'd
   137→		// poll a dead pane for the full timeout duration.
   138→		if !re.MatchString(content) {
   139→			if s.IsPaneDead() {
   140→				return content, fmt.Errorf("process exited while waiting for %q\n--- pane content ---\n%s\n--- end pane content ---", pattern, content)
   141→			}
   142→			// Pattern lost — reset
   143→			matchedAt = time.Time{}
   144→			lastStable = ""
   145→			time.Sleep(pollInterval)
   146→			continue
   147→		}
   148→
   149→		// Detect content change since Send was called
   150→		if !contentChanged && stable != s.stableAtSend {
   151→			contentChanged = true
   152→		}
   153→
   154→		if stable != lastStable {
   155→			// Pattern matches but content is still changing — reset settle timer
   156→			matchedAt = time.Now()
   157→			lastStable = stable
   158→			time.Sleep(pollInterval)
   159→			continue
   160→		}
   161→
   162→		// Pattern matches and content hasn't changed since matchedAt.
   163→		// Only settle if content changed at least once after Send
   164→		// (prevents false settle on echoed input before agent starts).
   165→		if contentChanged && time.Since(matchedAt) >= settleTime {
   166→			return content, nil
   167→		}
   168→
   169→		time.Sleep(pollInterval)
   170→	}
   171→	content := s.Capture()
   172→	return content, fmt.Errorf("timed out waiting for %q after %s\n--- pane content ---\n%s\n--- end pane content ---", pattern, timeout, content)
   173→}
   174→
   175→// IsPaneDead returns true if the process inside the tmux pane has exited.
   176→// This relies on the remain-on-exit option being set (which NewTmuxSession does).
   177→func (s *TmuxSession) IsPaneDead() bool {
   178→	cmd := exec.Command("tmux", "display-message", "-t", s.name, "-p", "#{pane_dead}")
   179→	out, err := cmd.Output()
   180→	if err != nil {
   181→		// If tmux itself fails (e.g. session/pane no longer exists),
   182→		// treat it as dead so WaitFor doesn't poll until timeout.
   183→		return true
   184→	}
   185→	return strings.TrimSpace(string(out)) == "1"
   186→}
   187→
   188→func (s *TmuxSession) Capture() string {
   189→	cmd := exec.Command("tmux", "capture-pane", "-t", s.name, "-p")
   190→	out, _ := cmd.Output()
   191→	return strings.TrimRight(string(out), "\n")
   192→}
   193→
   194→func (s *TmuxSession) Close() error {
   195→	for _, fn := range s.cleanups {
   196→		fn()
   197→	}
   198→	cmd := exec.Command("tmux", "kill-session", "-t", s.name)
   199→	out, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   200→	if err != nil {
   201→		return fmt.Errorf("tmux kill-session: %w\n%s", err, out)
   202→	}
   203→	return nil
   204→}
   205→
   206→// shellQuote wraps s in single quotes with proper escaping for POSIX shells.
   207→func shellQuote(s string) string {
   208→	return "'" + strings.ReplaceAll(s, "'", "'\\''") + "'"
   209→}
   210→
```

```
     1→package entire
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"encoding/json"
     5→	"log"
     6→	"os"
     7→	"os/exec"
     8→	"strings"
     9→	"testing"
    10→)
    11→
    12→// BinPath returns the path to the entire binary from E2E_ENTIRE_BIN.
    13→// The mise test:e2e tasks set this automatically via `mise run build`.
    14→func BinPath() string {
    15→	p := os.Getenv("E2E_ENTIRE_BIN")
    16→	if p == "" {
    17→		log.Fatal("entire: E2E_ENTIRE_BIN not set — run tests via `mise run test:e2e`")
    18→	}
    19→	return p
    20→}
    21→
    22→// RewindPoint represents a single entry from `entire rewind --list`.
    23→type RewindPoint struct {
    24→	ID             string `json:"id"`
    25→	Message        string `json:"message"`
    26→	MetadataDir    string `json:"metadata_dir"`
    27→	Date           string `json:"date"`
    28→	IsLogsOnly     bool   `json:"is_logs_only"`
    29→	CondensationID string `json:"condensation_id"`
    30→	SessionID      string `json:"session_id"`
    31→}
    32→
    33→// Enable runs `entire enable` for the given agent with telemetry disabled.
    34→func Enable(t *testing.T, dir, agent string) {
    35→	t.Helper()
    36→	run(t, dir, "enable", "--agent", agent, "--telemetry=false")
    37→}
    38→
    39→// Disable runs `entire disable` in the given directory.
    40→func Disable(t *testing.T, dir string) {
    41→	t.Helper()
    42→	run(t, dir, "disable")
    43→}
    44→
    45→// RewindList runs `entire rewind --list` and parses the JSON output.
    46→func RewindList(t *testing.T, dir string) []RewindPoint {
    47→	t.Helper()
    48→	out := run(t, dir, "rewind", "--list")
    49→
    50→	var points []RewindPoint
    51→	if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(out), &points); err != nil {
    52→		t.Fatalf("parse rewind list: %v\nraw output: %s", err, out)
    53→	}
    54→	return points
    55→}
    56→
    57→// Rewind runs `entire rewind --to <id>`. Returns an error instead of
    58→// failing the test, since callers may test failure cases.
    59→func Rewind(t *testing.T, dir, id string) error {
    60→	t.Helper()
    61→	return runErr(dir, "rewind", "--to", id)
    62→}
    63→
    64→// RewindLogsOnly runs `entire rewind --to <id> --logs-only`.
    65→func RewindLogsOnly(t *testing.T, dir, id string) error {
    66→	t.Helper()
    67→	return runErr(dir, "rewind", "--to", id, "--logs-only")
    68→}
    69→
    70→// run executes an `entire` subcommand in dir and fails the test on error.
    71→func run(t *testing.T, dir string, args ...string) string {
    72→	t.Helper()
    73→	cmd := exec.Command(BinPath(), args...)
    74→	cmd.Dir = dir
    75→	cmd.Env = append(os.Environ(), "ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=0")
    76→
    77→	out, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
    78→	if err != nil {
    79→		t.Fatalf("entire %s failed: %v\n%s", strings.Join(args, " "), err, out)
    80→	}
    81→	return strings.TrimSpace(string(out))
    82→}
    83→
    84→// runErr executes an `entire` subcommand in dir and returns any error.
    85→func runErr(dir string, args ...string) error {
    86→	cmd := exec.Command(BinPath(), args...)
    87→	cmd.Dir = dir
    88→	cmd.Env = append(os.Environ(), "ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=0")
    89→
    90→	out, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
    91→	if err != nil {
    92→		return &ExecError{
    93→			Args:   args,
    94→			Err:    err,
    95→			Output: string(out),
    96→		}
    97→	}
    98→	return nil
    99→}
   100→
   101→// ExecError wraps an entire CLI execution failure with its output.
   102→type ExecError struct {
   103→	Args   []string
   104→	Err    error
   105→	Output string
   106→}
   107→
   108→func (e *ExecError) Error() string {
   109→	return "entire " + strings.Join(e.Args, " ") + ": " + e.Err.Error() + "\n" + e.Output
   110→}
   111→
   112→func (e *ExecError) Unwrap() error {
   113→	return e.Err
   114→}
   115→
   116→// Explain runs `entire explain --checkpoint <id>` and returns the output.
   117→func Explain(t *testing.T, dir, checkpointID string) string {
   118→	t.Helper()
   119→	return run(t, dir, "explain", "--checkpoint", checkpointID)
   120→}
   121→
   122→// ExplainGenerate runs `entire explain --checkpoint <id> --generate`.
   123→// Returns (output, error) — doesn't fail test since callers may test failure cases.
   124→func ExplainGenerate(dir, checkpointID string) (string, error) {
   125→	return runOutput(dir, "explain", "--checkpoint", checkpointID, "--generate")
   126→}
   127→
   128→// ExplainCommit runs `entire explain --commit <ref>`.
   129→// Returns (output, error) — for testing failure cases.
   130→func ExplainCommit(dir, ref string) (string, error) {
   131→	return runOutput(dir, "explain", "--commit", ref)
   132→}
   133→
   134→// Resume runs `entire resume <branch> --force` and returns the output.
   135→func Resume(dir, branch string) (string, error) {
   136→	return runOutput(dir, "resume", branch, "--force")
   137→}
   138→
   139→// runOutput executes an `entire` subcommand and returns (output, error).
   140→func runOutput(dir string, args ...string) (string, error) {
   141→	cmd := exec.Command(BinPath(), args...)
   142→	cmd.Dir = dir
   143→	cmd.Env = append(os.Environ(), "ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=0")
   144→
   145→	out, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   146→	if err != nil {
   147→		return string(out), &ExecError{
   148→			Args:   args,
   149→			Err:    err,
   150→			Output: string(out),
   151→		}
   152→	}
   153→	return strings.TrimSpace(string(out)), nil
   154→}
   155→
```

```
     1→package testutil
     2→
     3→import "time"
     4→
     5→type TokenUsage struct {
     6→	InputTokens         int `json:"input_tokens"`
     7→	CacheCreationTokens int `json:"cache_creation_tokens"`
     8→	CacheReadTokens     int `json:"cache_read_tokens"`
     9→	OutputTokens        int `json:"output_tokens"`
    10→	APICallCount        int `json:"api_call_count"`

    12→
    13→type Attribution struct {
    14→	CalculatedAt    time.Time `json:"calculated_at"`
    15→	AgentLines      int       `json:"agent_lines"`
    16→	HumanAdded      int       `json:"human_added"`
    17→	HumanModified   int       `json:"human_modified"`
    18→	HumanRemoved    int       `json:"human_removed"`
    19→	TotalCommitted  int       `json:"total_committed"`
    20→	AgentPercentage float64   `json:"agent_percentage"`
    21→}
    22→
    23→type CheckpointMetadata struct {
    24→	CLIVersion       string       `json:"cli_version"`
    25→	CheckpointID     string       `json:"checkpoint_id"`
    26→	Strategy         string       `json:"strategy"`
    27→	Branch           string       `json:"branch"`
    28→	CheckpointsCount int          `json:"checkpoints_count"`
    29→	FilesTouched     []string     `json:"files_touched"`
    30→	Sessions         []SessionRef `json:"sessions"`
    31→	TokenUsage       TokenUsage   `json:"token_usage"`
    32→}
    33→
    34→type SessionRef struct {
    35→	Metadata    string `json:"metadata"`
    36→	Transcript  string `json:"transcript"`
    37→	Context     string `json:"context"`
    38→	ContentHash string `json:"content_hash"`
    39→	Prompt      string `json:"prompt"`
    40→}
    41→
    42→type SessionMetadata struct {
    43→	CLIVersion         string      `json:"cli_version"`
    44→	CheckpointID       string      `json:"checkpoint_id"`
    45→	SessionID          string      `json:"session_id"`
    46→	Strategy           string      `json:"strategy"`
    47→	CreatedAt          time.Time   `json:"created_at"`
    48→	Branch             string      `json:"branch"`
    49→	Agent              string      `json:"agent"`
    50→	CheckpointsCount   int         `json:"checkpoints_count"`
    51→	FilesTouched       []string    `json:"files_touched"`
    52→	TokenUsage         TokenUsage  `json:"token_usage"`
    53→	InitialAttribution Attribution `json:"initial_attribution"`
    54→	TranscriptPath     string      `json:"transcript_path"`
    55→}
    56→
    57→func CheckpointPath(id string) string {
    58→	return id[:2] + "/" + id[2:]
    59→}
```

```
     1→package testutil
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"fmt"
     5→	"os"
     6→	"os/exec"
     7→	"path/filepath"
     8→	"regexp"
     9→	"strconv"
    10→	"strings"
    11→	"testing"
    12→	"time"
    13→)
    14→
    15→// ArtifactRoot is the absolute path to the artifact output directory.
    16→// Must be set in TestMain before any tests run.
    17→var ArtifactRoot string
    18→
    19→// ArtifactTimestamp is the timestamp subdirectory for this test run.
    20→var ArtifactTimestamp = time.Now().Format("2006-01-02T15-04-05")
    21→
    22→var runDirOverride string
    23→
    24→// SetRunDir overrides the artifact run directory (e.g. from E2E_ARTIFACT_DIR).
    25→func SetRunDir(dir string) {
    26→	runDirOverride = dir
    27→}
    28→
    29→// ArtifactRunDir returns the directory for the current test run.
    30→func ArtifactRunDir() string {
    31→	if runDirOverride != "" {
    32→		return runDirOverride
    33→	}
    34→	return filepath.Join(ArtifactRoot, ArtifactTimestamp)
    35→}
    36→
    37→func artifactDir(t *testing.T) string {
    38→	t.Helper()
    39→	name := strings.ReplaceAll(t.Name(), "/", "-")
    40→	dir := filepath.Join(ArtifactRunDir(), name)
    41→	if err := os.MkdirAll(dir, 0o755); err != nil {
    42→		t.Logf("warning: failed to create artifact dir: %v", err)
    43→	}
    44→	return dir
    45→}
    46→
    47→// CaptureArtifacts captures git state, checkpoint metadata, entire logs,
    48→// and console output to the artifact directory.
    49→func CaptureArtifacts(t *testing.T, s *RepoState) {
    50→	t.Helper()
    51→	dir := s.ArtifactDir
    52→
    53→	writeArtifact(t, dir, "git-log.txt",
    54→		gitOutputSafe(s.Dir, "log", "--decorate", "--graph", "--all"))
    55→
    56→	writeArtifact(t, dir, "git-tree.txt",
    57→		gitOutputSafe(s.Dir, "ls-tree", "-r", "HEAD")+
    58→			"\n--- entire/checkpoints/v1 ---\n"+
    59→			gitOutputSafe(s.Dir, "ls-tree", "-r", "entire/checkpoints/v1"))
    60→
    61→	// console.log is written incrementally to disk via s.ConsoleLog (*os.File),
    62→	// so it already exists in the artifact dir and survives global timeouts.
    63→
    64→	// Capture final pane content from interactive sessions, then close.
    65→	// Must happen here (not in a separate t.Cleanup) because cleanup
    66→	// functions run LIFO — closing first would kill the tmux session
    67→	// before we can capture.
    68→	if s.session != nil {
    69→		pane := s.session.Capture()
    70→		writeArtifact(t, dir, "pane.txt", pane)
    71→		_ = s.session.Close()
    72→	}
    73→
    74→	if t.Failed() {
    75→		writeArtifact(t, dir, "FAIL", "")
    76→	} else {
    77→		writeArtifact(t, dir, "PASS", "")
    78→	}
    79→
    80→	captureCheckpointMetadata(t, s, dir)
    81→	captureEntireLogs(t, s.Dir, dir)
    82→
    83→	if os.Getenv("E2E_KEEP_REPOS") != "" {
    84→		link := filepath.Join(dir, "repo")
    85→		if err := os.Symlink(s.Dir, link); err != nil {
    86→			t.Logf("warning: failed to symlink repo: %v", err)
    87→		}
    88→	}
    89→}
    90→
    91→func captureCheckpointMetadata(t *testing.T, s *RepoState, outDir string) {
    92→	t.Helper()
    93→	commits := NewCheckpointCommits(t, s)
    94→	metaDir := filepath.Join(outDir, "checkpoint-metadata")
    95→
    96→	cpIDRe := regexp.MustCompile(`(?m)^Checkpoint:\s+(\S+)`)
    97→	for _, sha := range commits {
    98→		msg := gitOutputSafe(s.Dir, "log", "-1", "--format=%B", sha)
    99→		m := cpIDRe.FindStringSubmatch(msg)
   100→		if m == nil || len(m[1]) < 3 {
   101→			continue
   102→		}
   103→		id := m[1]
   104→		cpPath := CheckpointPath(id)
   105→
   106→		cpDir := filepath.Join(metaDir, cpPath)
   107→		_ = os.MkdirAll(cpDir, 0o755)
   108→		raw := gitOutputSafe(s.Dir, "show", sha+":"+cpPath+"/metadata.json")
   109→		writeArtifact(t, cpDir, "metadata.json", raw)
   110→
   111→		for i := 0; ; i++ {
   112→			sessPath := fmt.Sprintf("%s/%d/metadata.json", cpPath, i)
   113→			raw := gitOutputSafe(s.Dir, "show", sha+":"+sessPath)
   114→			if raw == "" {
   115→				break
   116→			}
   117→			sessDir := filepath.Join(cpDir, strconv.Itoa(i))
   118→			_ = os.MkdirAll(sessDir, 0o755)
   119→			writeArtifact(t, sessDir, "metadata.json", raw)
   120→		}
   121→	}
   122→}
   123→
   124→func captureEntireLogs(t *testing.T, repoDir, outDir string) {
   125→	t.Helper()
   126→	logDir := filepath.Join(repoDir, ".entire", "logs")
   127→	entries, err := os.ReadDir(logDir)
   128→	if err != nil {
   129→		return
   130→	}
   131→	dst := filepath.Join(outDir, "entire-logs")
   132→	_ = os.MkdirAll(dst, 0o755)
   133→	for _, e := range entries {
   134→		data, err := os.ReadFile(filepath.Join(logDir, e.Name()))
   135→		if err != nil {
   136→			continue
   137→		}
   138→		writeArtifact(t, dst, e.Name(), string(data))
   139→	}
   140→}
   141→
   142→func writeArtifact(t *testing.T, dir, name, content string) {
   143→	t.Helper()
   144→	path := filepath.Join(dir, name)
   145→	if err := os.WriteFile(path, []byte(content), 0o644); err != nil {
   146→		t.Logf("warning: failed to write artifact %s: %v", path, err)
   147→	}
   148→}
   149→
   150→func gitOutputSafe(dir string, args ...string) string {
   151→	cmd := exec.Command("git", args...)
   152→	cmd.Dir = dir
   153→	out, err := cmd.Output()
   154→	if err != nil {
   155→		return ""
   156→	}
   157→	return string(out)
   158→}
   159→
```

```
     1→package testutil
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"encoding/json"
     6→	"errors"
     7→	"fmt"
     8→	"os"
     9→	"os/exec"
    10→	"path/filepath"
    11→	"strings"
    12→	"testing"
    13→	"time"
    14→
    15→	"github.com/entireio/cli/e2e/agents"
    16→	"github.com/entireio/cli/e2e/entire"
    17→)
    18→
    19→const droidRepoSettingsPath = ".factory/settings.json"
    20→
    21→// RepoState holds the working state for a single test's cloned repository.
    22→type RepoState struct {
    23→	Agent            agents.Agent
    24→	Dir              string
    25→	ArtifactDir      string
    26→	HeadBefore       string
    27→	CheckpointBefore string
    28→	ConsoleLog       *os.File
    29→	session          agents.Session // interactive session, if started via StartSession
    30→	skipArtifacts    bool           // suppresses artifact capture on scenario restart
    31→}
    32→
    33→// SetupRepo creates a fresh git repository in a temporary directory, seeds it
    34→// with an initial commit, and runs `entire enable` for the given agent.
    35→// Artifact capture is registered as a cleanup function.
    36→//
    37→// When E2E_KEEP_REPOS is set, the temporary directory is not cleaned up
    38→// so it can be inspected after the test. A symlink in the artifact dir
    39→// points to the preserved repo.
    40→func SetupRepo(t *testing.T, agent agents.Agent) *RepoState {
    41→	t.Helper()
    42→
    43→	keepRepos := os.Getenv("E2E_KEEP_REPOS") != ""
    44→
    45→	// Always use os.MkdirTemp instead of t.TempDir(). Go's t.TempDir()
    46→	// creates nested subdirectories (TestName.../001/) whose structure
    47→	// confuses some agents' (e.g. opencode) working-directory resolution.
    48→	dir, err := os.MkdirTemp("", "e2e-repo-*")
    49→	if err != nil {
    50→		t.Fatalf("create temp dir: %v", err)
    51→	}
    52→	if keepRepos {
    53→		t.Logf("E2E_KEEP_REPOS: repo will be preserved at %s", dir)
    54→	} else {
    55→		t.Cleanup(func() { os.RemoveAll(dir) })
    56→	}
    57→
    58→	// Resolve symlinks (macOS: /var -> /private/var) so paths match
    59→	// what agent CLIs see when they resolve their own CWD.
    60→	if resolved, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(dir); err == nil {
    61→		dir = resolved
    62→	}
    63→
    64→	Git(t, dir, "init")
    65→	Git(t, dir, "config", "user.name", "E2E Test")
    66→	Git(t, dir, "config", "user.email", "e2e@test.local")
    67→	Git(t, dir, "commit", "--allow-empty", "-m", "initial commit")
    68→
    69→	// External agents need external_agents enabled in settings before enable,
    70→	// so the CLI can discover the agent binary via PATH during DiscoverAndRegister.
    71→	if ea, ok := agent.(agents.ExternalAgent); ok && ea.IsExternalAgent() {
    72→		entireDir := filepath.Join(dir, ".entire")
    73→		if err := os.MkdirAll(entireDir, 0o755); err != nil {
    74→			t.Fatalf("create .entire for external agent: %v", err)
    75→		}
    76→		if err := os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(entireDir, "settings.json"),
    77→			[]byte("{\"external_agents\": true}\n"), 0o644); err != nil {
    78→			t.Fatalf("write external_agents setting: %v", err)
    79→		}
    80→	}
    81→
    82→	entire.Enable(t, dir, agent.EntireAgent())
    83→	if agent.Name() == "factoryai-droid" {
    84→		if err := configureDroidRepoSettings(dir); err != nil {
    85→			t.Fatalf("configure droid repo settings: %v", err)
    86→		}
    87→	}
    88→	PatchSettings(t, dir, map[string]any{"log_level": "debug"})
    89→
    90→	// Copilot CLI blocks on a "No copilot instructions found" notice in fresh
    91→	// repos that lack .github/copilot-instructions.md, preventing the interactive
    92→	// prompt from appearing.
    93→	if agent.Name() == "copilot-cli" {
    94→		ghDir := filepath.Join(dir, ".github")
    95→		if err := os.MkdirAll(ghDir, 0o755); err != nil {
    96→			t.Fatalf("create .github dir: %v", err)
    97→		}
    98→		if err := os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(ghDir, "copilot-instructions.md"), []byte("# E2E Test\n"), 0o644); err != nil {
    99→			t.Fatalf("write copilot-instructions.md: %v", err)
   100→		}
   101→	}
   102→
   103→	// OpenCode's non-interactive mode auto-rejects external_directory permission
   104→	// since there's no user to prompt. Write a config to allow it.
   105→	if agent.Name() == "opencode" {
   106→		cfg := `{"$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json", "permission": {"external_directory": "allow"}}`
   107→		if key := os.Getenv("ANTHROPIC_API_KEY"); key != "" {
   108→			cfg = fmt.Sprintf(`{"$schema": "https://opencode.ai/config.json", "permission": {"external_directory": "allow"}, "provider": {"anthropic": {"options": {"apiKey": %q}}}}`, key)
   109→		}
   110→		if err := os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(dir, "opencode.json"), []byte(cfg+"\n"), 0o644); err != nil {
   111→			t.Fatalf("write opencode.json: %v", err)
   112→		}
   113→	}
   114→
   115→	// Create artifact dir eagerly so console.log is written to disk
   116→	// incrementally. Even if the test is killed by a global timeout,
   117→	// partial output survives.
   118→	artDir := artifactDir(t)
   119→	consoleLog, err := os.Create(filepath.Join(artDir, "console.log"))
   120→	if err != nil {
   121→		t.Fatalf("create console.log: %v", err)
   122→	}
   123→
   124→	state := &RepoState{
   125→		Agent:            agent,
   126→		Dir:              dir,
   127→		ArtifactDir:      artDir,
   128→		HeadBefore:       GitOutput(t, dir, "rev-parse", "HEAD"),
   129→		CheckpointBefore: GitOutput(t, dir, "rev-parse", "entire/checkpoints/v1"),
   130→		ConsoleLog:       consoleLog,
   131→	}
   132→
   133→	t.Cleanup(func() {
   134→		_ = consoleLog.Close()
   135→		if !state.skipArtifacts {
   136→			CaptureArtifacts(t, state)
   137→		}
   138→	})
   139→
   140→	return state
   141→}
   142→
   143→func configureDroidRepoSettings(repoDir string) error {
   144→	home, err := os.UserHomeDir()
   145→	if err != nil {
   146→		return fmt.Errorf("get home dir: %w", err)
   147→	}
   148→
   149→	globalSettingsPath := filepath.Join(home, ".factory", "settings.json")
   150→	repoSettingsPath := filepath.Join(repoDir, droidRepoSettingsPath)
   151→
   152→	if err := mergeDroidCustomModels(globalSettingsPath, repoSettingsPath); err != nil {
   153→		return err
   154→	}
   155→	if err := ensureGitInfoExcludeContains(repoDir, droidRepoSettingsPath); err != nil {
   156→		return fmt.Errorf("exclude droid settings from git: %w", err)
   157→	}
   158→	return nil
   159→}
   160→
   161→func mergeDroidCustomModels(globalSettingsPath, repoSettingsPath string) error {
   162→	globalSettings, err := loadJSONMap(globalSettingsPath, "global droid settings")
   163→	if err != nil {
   164→		return err
   165→	}
   166→
   167→	customModels, ok := globalSettings["customModels"]
   168→	if !ok {
   169→		return fmt.Errorf(
   170→			"global droid settings at %s missing customModels; repo-local %s shadows global settings",
   171→			globalSettingsPath,
   172→			repoSettingsPath,
   173→		)
   174→	}
   175→
   176→	var models []json.RawMessage
   177→	if err := json.Unmarshal(customModels, &models); err != nil {
   178→		return fmt.Errorf("parse customModels in %s: %w", globalSettingsPath, err)
   179→	}
   180→	if len(models) == 0 {
   181→		return fmt.Errorf("global droid settings at %s has empty customModels", globalSettingsPath)
   182→	}
   183→
   184→	repoSettings, err := loadJSONMap(repoSettingsPath, "repo-local droid settings")
   185→	if err != nil {
   186→		return err
   187→	}
   188→	repoSettings["customModels"] = customModels
   189→
   190→	// Set the active custom model so interactive mode uses the BYOK model
   191→	// instead of prompting for selection. The value must match the Model
   192→	// field from the customModels entry (not the displayName).
   193→	modelJSON, err := json.Marshal(agents.DefaultDroidModel())
   194→	if err != nil {
   195→		return fmt.Errorf("marshal model setting: %w", err)
   196→	}
   197→	repoSettings["model"] = json.RawMessage(modelJSON)
   198→
   199→	// High autonomy prevents Droid from asking for confirmation during
   200→	// interactive E2E tests, which would cause hangs waiting for input.
   201→	repoSettings["autonomyMode"] = json.RawMessage(`"auto-high"`)
   202→	repoSettings["completionSound"] = json.RawMessage(`"off"`)
   203→
   204→	out, err := json.MarshalIndent(repoSettings, "", "  ")
   205→	if err != nil {
   206→		return fmt.Errorf("marshal repo-local droid settings: %w", err)
   207→	}
   208→	out = append(out, '\n')
   209→
   210→	if err := os.WriteFile(repoSettingsPath, out, 0o600); err != nil {
   211→		return fmt.Errorf("write repo-local droid settings %s: %w", repoSettingsPath, err)
   212→	}
   213→	return nil
   214→}
   215→
   216→func loadJSONMap(path, description string) (map[string]json.RawMessage, error) {
   217→	data, err := os.ReadFile(path)
   218→	if err != nil {
   219→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("read %s at %s: %w", description, path, err)
   220→	}
   221→
   222→	var parsed map[string]json.RawMessage
   223→	if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &parsed); err != nil {
   224→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("parse %s at %s: %w", description, path, err)
   225→	}
   226→	if parsed == nil {
   227→		parsed = make(map[string]json.RawMessage)
   228→	}
   229→	return parsed, nil
   230→}
   231→
   232→func ensureGitInfoExcludeContains(repoDir, entry string) error {
   233→	excludePath := filepath.Join(repoDir, ".git", "info", "exclude")
   234→
   235→	data, err := os.ReadFile(excludePath)
   236→	if err != nil && !errors.Is(err, os.ErrNotExist) {
   237→		return fmt.Errorf("read %s: %w", excludePath, err)
   238→	}
   239→
   240→	normalized := strings.ReplaceAll(string(data), "\r\n", "\n")
   241→	for _, line := range strings.Split(normalized, "\n") {
   242→		if strings.TrimSpace(line) == entry {
   243→			return nil
   244→		}
   245→	}
   246→
   247→	var b strings.Builder
   248→	if len(data) > 0 {
   249→		b.Write(data)
   250→		if data[len(data)-1] != '\n' {
   251→			b.WriteByte('\n')
   252→		}
   253→	}
   254→	b.WriteString(entry)
   255→	b.WriteByte('\n')
   256→
   257→	if err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Dir(excludePath), 0o755); err != nil {
   258→		return fmt.Errorf("create %s: %w", filepath.Dir(excludePath), err)
   259→	}
   260→	if err := os.WriteFile(excludePath, []byte(b.String()), 0o644); err != nil {
   261→		return fmt.Errorf("write %s: %w", excludePath, err)
   262→	}
   263→	return nil
   264→}
   265→
   266→// ForEachAgent runs fn as a parallel subtest for every registered agent.
   267→// It handles repo setup, concurrency gating, context timeout, and cleanup.
   268→// The timeout is scaled by each agent's TimeoutMultiplier.
   269→//
   270→// If RunPrompt detects a transient API error (e.g. rate limit, token refresh
   271→// failure), it panics with errScenarioRestart. ForEachAgent recovers from the
   272→// panic and restarts the entire scenario with a fresh repository, up to
   273→// maxScenarioRestarts times. This avoids stale CLI session state from the
   274→// failed attempt poisoning the retry.
   275→func ForEachAgent(t *testing.T, timeout time.Duration, fn func(t *testing.T, s *RepoState, ctx context.Context)) {
   276→	t.Helper()
   277→	t.Parallel()
   278→	all := agents.All()
   279→	if len(all) == 0 {
   280→		t.Skip("no agents registered (check E2E_AGENT filter)")
   281→	}
   282→	for _, agent := range all {
   283→		t.Run(agent.Name(), func(t *testing.T) {
   284→			// Use the global test deadline for slot wait so we don't
   285→			// skip prematurely — only bail if the whole binary is dying.
   286→			slotCtx := context.Background()
   287→			if deadline, ok := t.Deadline(); ok {
   288→				var cancel context.CancelFunc
   289→				slotCtx, cancel = context.WithDeadline(slotCtx, deadline)
   290→				defer cancel()
   291→			}
   292→			if err := agents.AcquireSlot(slotCtx, agent); err != nil {
   293→				t.Fatalf("timed out waiting for agent slot: %v", err)
   294→			}
   295→			defer agents.ReleaseSlot(agent)
   296→
   297→			// Per-test timeout starts after slot is acquired, scaled
   298→			// by the agent's multiplier (e.g. 2.5× for gemini).
   299→			scaled := time.Duration(float64(timeout) * agent.TimeoutMultiplier())
   300→
   301→			var prevState *RepoState
   302→			for attempt := range maxScenarioRestarts + 1 {
   303→				s := SetupRepo(t, agent)
   304→				ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), scaled)
   305→
   306→				// On restart, suppress artifact capture from the previous
   307→				// failed attempt so it doesn't overwrite the final one.
   308→				if prevState != nil {
   309→					prevState.skipArtifacts = true
   310→				}
   311→
   312→				restarted := runScenario(t, s, ctx, fn)
   313→				cancel()
   314→
   315→				if !restarted {
   316→					return
   317→				}
   318→				prevState = s
   319→				if attempt >= maxScenarioRestarts {
   320→					t.Fatalf("exhausted %d scenario attempts due to transient API errors", maxScenarioRestarts+1)
   321→				}
   322→				t.Logf("transient error, restarting scenario (attempt %d/%d)", attempt+2, maxScenarioRestarts+1)
   323→			}
   324→		})
   325→	}
   326→}
   327→
   328→// runScenario runs the test function and recovers from errScenarioRestart
   329→// panics triggered by transient API errors in RunPrompt. Returns true if
   330→// the scenario should be restarted with a fresh repository.
   331→func runScenario(t *testing.T, s *RepoState, ctx context.Context, fn func(t *testing.T, s *RepoState, ctx context.Context)) (restarted bool) {
   332→	t.Helper()
   333→	defer func() {
   334→		if r := recover(); r != nil {
   335→			if _, ok := r.(errScenarioRestart); ok {
   336→				restarted = true
   337→				return
   338→			}
   339→			panic(r) // re-panic for unexpected panics
   340→		}
   341→	}()
   342→	fn(t, s, ctx)
   343→	return false
   344→}
   345→
   346→const maxScenarioRestarts = 2 // restart up to 2 times = 3 total attempts
   347→
   348→// errScenarioRestart is panicked by RunPrompt when a transient API error is
   349→// detected. ForEachAgent recovers from this panic and restarts the test
   350→// scenario with a fresh repository, avoiding stale CLI state from the failed
   351→// attempt.
   352→type errScenarioRestart struct {
   353→	msg string
   354→}
   355→
   356→// RunPrompt runs an agent prompt, logs the command and output to ConsoleLog,
   357→// and returns the result. If the agent reports a transient API error, it
   358→// panics with errScenarioRestart to trigger a full scenario restart in
   359→// ForEachAgent (see runScenario).
   360→func (s *RepoState) RunPrompt(t *testing.T, ctx context.Context, prompt string, opts ...agents.Option) (agents.Output, error) {
   361→	t.Helper()
   362→	out, err := s.Agent.RunPrompt(ctx, s.Dir, prompt, opts...)
   363→	s.logPromptResult(out)
   364→
   365→	if err != nil && s.Agent.IsTransientError(out, err) {
   366→		errMsg := fmt.Sprintf("transient API error (stderr: %s)", strings.TrimSpace(out.Stderr))
   367→		t.Logf("%s — restarting scenario", errMsg)
   368→		fmt.Fprintf(s.ConsoleLog, "> [transient] %s — restarting scenario\n", errMsg)
   369→		panic(errScenarioRestart{msg: errMsg})
   370→	}
   371→
   372→	return out, err
   373→}
   374→
   375→func (s *RepoState) logPromptResult(out agents.Output) {
   376→	s.ConsoleLog.WriteString("> " + out.Command + "\n")
   377→	s.ConsoleLog.WriteString("stdout:\n" + out.Stdout + "\n")
   378→	s.ConsoleLog.WriteString("stderr:\n" + out.Stderr + "\n")
   379→}
   380→
   381→// Git runs a git command in the repo and logs it to ConsoleLog.
   382→func (s *RepoState) Git(t *testing.T, args ...string) {
   383→	t.Helper()
   384→	s.ConsoleLog.WriteString("> git " + strings.Join(args, " ") + "\n")
   385→	Git(t, s.Dir, args...)
   386→}
   387→
   388→// StartSession starts an interactive session and registers it for pane
   389→// capture in artifacts. Returns nil if the agent does not support interactive
   390→// mode. The session is closed automatically during test cleanup.
   391→func (s *RepoState) StartSession(t *testing.T, ctx context.Context) agents.Session {
   392→	t.Helper()
   393→	session, err := s.Agent.StartSession(ctx, s.Dir)
   394→	if err != nil {
   395→		t.Fatalf("start session: %v", err)
   396→	}
   397→	if session == nil {
   398→		return nil
   399→	}
   400→	s.session = session
   401→	return session
   402→}

   432→// PatchSettings merges extra keys into .entire/settings.json.
   433→func PatchSettings(t *testing.T, dir string, extra map[string]any) {
   434→	t.Helper()

   444→	for k, v := range extra {

   448→	if err != nil {

   451→	if err := os.WriteFile(path, out, 0o644); err != nil {

   456→// Git runs a git command in the given directory and fails the test if it
   457→// returns a non-zero exit code.
   458→func Git(t *testing.T, dir string, args ...string) {
   459→	t.Helper()
   460→
   461→	cmd := exec.Command("git", args...)
   462→	if dir != "" {
   463→		cmd.Dir = dir
   464→	}
   465→	cmd.Env = append(os.Environ(), "ENTIRE_TEST_TTY=0")
   466→
   467→	out, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()
   468→	if err != nil {
   469→		t.Fatalf("git %s failed: %v\n%s", strings.Join(args, " "), err, out)
   470→	}
   471→}
   472→
   473→// GitOutput runs a git command in the given directory, returns its trimmed
   474→// stdout, and fails the test on error.
   475→func GitOutput(t *testing.T, dir string, args ...string) string {
   476→	t.Helper()
   477→
   478→	cmd := exec.Command("git", args...)
   479→	if dir != "" {
   480→		cmd.Dir = dir
   481→	}
   482→
   483→	out, err := cmd.Output()
   484→	if err != nil {
   485→		var stderr string
   486→		ee := &exec.ExitError{}
   487→		if errors.As(err, &ee) {
   488→			stderr = string(ee.Stderr)
   489→		}
   490→		t.Fatalf("git %s failed: %v\n%s", strings.Join(args, " "), err, stderr)
   491→	}
   492→
   493→	return strings.TrimSpace(string(out))
   494→}
```
